\addfontfeature
Language=English generated per installation hinder cross-installation correlation based on identifiers alone.

Access Control and Security Measures

Database Security

ArangoDB provides several security features that the UPI leverages:

Authentication:

Username/password authentication with token-based sessions,

Authorization:

Role-based access control to collections and operations,

Encryption:

TLS for client-server communication, encryption at rest (enterprise edition), and

Audit logging:

Detailed logs of all database operations.

Local Deployment Architecture

Access control in
Indaleko
implements technical safeguards appropriate to different deployment architectures:

Least Privilege Implementation:

The system creates a separate database account with minimal required permissions after initial setup.

Local Deployment Architecture:

Default configuration assumes local hosting to maintain complete control over memory pattern data.

Process Isolation:

Computational operations occur within the deployment boundary to minimize external data exposure.

Token Management:

Authentication tokens for external services follow platform-specific secure storage mechanisms.

Permission Boundaries:

Each collector operates with minimal required permissions for its specific data source.

External Service Mitigation

Interacting with external services, especially LLMs, introduces data exposure risks. Mitigation strategies include:

Local LLM/SLM Fallback:

Avoids external calls entirely when available and selected.

Query Anonymization and Rotation:

Round-robin rotation distributes requests across providers.

Non-Data-Preserving Proxies:

Network connectivity uses services like ngrok for data forwarding without storage.

Key Management Architecture

The architecture implements a hierarchical key management system:

Master Key Control

Users control a master key that derives all other encryption keys, ensuring:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

No vendor lock-in,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Portable data migration,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Complete user control, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Key recovery mechanisms

Key Hierarchy

Master key:

User-controlled, derives all other keys,

Schema key:

Encrypts UUID-to-semantic mappings,

Collection keys:

Encrypt entire collections (Layer 2), and

Attribute keys:

Per-field encryption (Layer 3).

Current Implementation Status

The current security posture of
Indaleko
provides a baseline of privacy through semantic decoupling (Layer 0), local-first principles, and standard token management. This implementation demonstrates the feasibility of the approach while providing a foundation for future enhancements.

Currently implemented security features focus on:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Semantic decoupling through UUID-based metadata representation,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Simple access control for database operations,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Token management for external service authentication, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Local-first implementation that prioritizes user control.

Implementation Challenges

Important privacy challenges addressed during
Indaleko
implementation included:

Context Privacy:

Memory Anchor collection could potentially capture sensitive information. Addressed through filtering, aggregation, and user control over context capture.

External Service Dependencies:

Some capabilities require external services that could compromise privacy. Mitigated through local alternatives, data minimization, and transparent policies.

Identity Correlation:

Maintaining privacy while enabling cross-service entity resolution. Solved through the UUID-based privacy model with controlled linking.

Permission Management:

Ensuring appropriate access levels for different data sources. Implemented a fine-grained permission system with explicit user consent flows.

Developer Experience:

Security aspects that increase protection also make debugging more difficult. Addressed through git pre-commit hooks to enforce security practices.

Data Protection by Asset Type

Different types of data within the UPI require specific privacy considerations:

Memory Anchor Data

Activity stream data forms a distinctive category of personal information with unique privacy characteristics.

Privacy Challenges

Continuous generation:

Users generate activity streams throughout their daily lives,

Rich inference potential:

Patterns can reveal habits, preferences, and private behaviors,

Temporal sensitivity:

Recent activity stream often more sensitive than historical data,

Cross-device correlation:

Activity patterns can be linked across multiple devices,

Location sensitivity:

Spatial context can reveal home, work, and frequently visited locations.

Privacy-Preserving Design Principles

Granular control:

Users can selectively enable/disable capture of specific activity types,

Temporal boundaries:

Automatic expiration of activity stream after user-defined periods,

Context anonymization:

Stripping identifying information from activity records where possible,

Local processing:

Activity analysis performed on-device without cloud dependencies, and

Aggregation over raw data:

Storing statistical summaries rather than detailed event logs.

Specific Protections

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Location data undergoes precision reduction based on context sensitivity,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Application usage patterns aggregated to time windows rather than precise timestamps,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Device identifiers replaced with user-controlled aliases,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Network identifiers hashed to prevent external correlation, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Activity patterns analyzed locally with only derived metadata retained.

Query Logs

Query history requires special protection as it directly reflects user interests and information needs:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Queries stored with timestamps rounded to reduce temporal correlation,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Natural language queries processed locally before any external API calls,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Query patterns analyzed for system improvement without retaining raw queries, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

User option to disable query logging entirely.

File Metadata

Standard file metadata receives baseline protection through semantic decoupling:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Path information separated from content identifiers,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Creation/modification times stored with configurable precision,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Owner information replaced with user-controlled identifiers, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

File type associations masked through UUID mapping.

Production Deployment Considerations

Note that the privacy design principles described throughout this appendix are readily accomplished in the proof-of-concept system. However, maintaining these principles in a production environment will require additional consideration:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Users may choose to use no or low cost services in exchange for sharing their data with third party providers,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Production systems must balance privacy protections with performance and scalability requirements,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Commercial deployments may face additional constraints from business models that rely on data monetization, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Regulatory compliance may introduce additional privacy requirements beyond those in the prototype.

Future Research Directions

Several areas warrant continued research:

Formal verification:

Mathematical proofs of privacy properties,

User studies:

Understanding privacy preferences and usability of controls,

Regulatory compliance:

Adapting to GDPR, CCPA, and emerging regulations,

Privacy-preserving query languages:

Development of query systems that inherently protect privacy,

Decentralized identity systems:

Integration with emerging identity standards,

Adaptive privacy:

Systems that learn and adapt to user privacy preferences, and

Privacy measurement:

Metrics for quantifying privacy protection effectiveness.

Future Privacy Architecture Research

Future architectural research will advance privacy-preserving memory models through:

Encrypted episodic pattern storage:

Advanced encryption schemes for complex memory patterns,

Zero-knowledge memory retrieval protocols:

Proving memory existence without revealing content,

Hierarchical privacy boundaries:

Multi-level privacy controls within distributed memory systems, and

Three-layer implementation model:

Full deployment of the proposed privacy architecture.

The proposed three-layer model for future systems includes:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Encrypted memory patterns stored via UUIDs,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Separate semantic mapping tables with independent encryption, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Abstraction layers that decouple meaning from storage.

Future research will address cryptographic key management for memory patterns, implement access auditing mechanisms, and develop secure staging environments that maintain semantic boundaries. Integration with the Pydantic-based serialization architecture is progressing. This model enables controlled memory pattern sharing through mapping and cryptographic access control.

Conclusion

The UPI’s privacy architecture balances practical usability with strong privacy protections through a local-first design philosophy. While the current implementation provides basic privacy features, the framework supports evolution toward more sophisticated privacy-preserving technologies as they mature.

The layered approach allows users to choose appropriate privacy levels based on their threat model and performance requirements. As privacy-preserving techniques advance, the UPI architecture can incorporate new methods while maintaining its core principle: personal information should remain under personal control.

Key achievements include:

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Demonstration of semantic decoupling as a practical privacy technique,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Implementation of local-first architecture for Memory Anchors,

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Flexible security model adaptable to different deployment scenarios, and

\fontspec_if_language:nT
ENG
\addfontfeature
Language=English•

Foundation for advanced privacy features in future versions.

The privacy model described here reflects our commitment to user control and data protection while acknowledging the practical constraints of current technology. Future work will focus on formal security analysis, performance optimization, and user-friendly privacy management interfaces.
